`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

className=\\"h-3.5 w-3.5\\" strokeWidth={2} />\\r\\n          Assign work\\r\\n        </button>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <section aria-label=\\"Guided drills and Activities\\" className=\\"space-y-2 px-4 py-2\\">\\r\\n        <h2 className=\\"text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider text-muted-foreground\\">\\r\\n          Guided drills and Activities\\r\\n        </h2>\\r\\n        {ownedWork.resolution.state === \'denied\' ? (\\r\\n          <div role=\\"alert\\" className=\\"text-xs text-destructive\\">\\r\\n            This student view is unavailable to this account.\\r\\n          </div>\\r\\n        ) : (\\r\\n          <>\\r\\n            {ownedWork.error && (\\r\\n              <div role=\\"alert\\" className=\\"text-xs text-destructive\\">\\r\\n                Could not refresh guided drills and Activities. Earlier saved work is still shown when available.\\r\\n                <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"ml-2 underline\\" onClick={() => void ownedWork.refresh()}>Try again</button>\\r\\n              </div>\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n            {ownedWork.failedSources.length > 0 && (\\r\\n              <div role=\\"alert\\" className=\\"text-xs text-destructive\\">\\r\\n                Could not confirm {ownedWork.failedSources.join(\', \')}. (\\r\\n              <ul className=\\"space-y-1.5\\">\\r\\n                {ownedWork.rows.map((row) => (\\r\\n                  <li key={`${row.sourceTable}:${row.refId}`} className=\\"flex items-start justify-between gap-3 rounded-lg border border-border/40 bg-card/40 px-3 py-2.5\\">\\r\\n                    <div className=\\"min-w-0\\">\\r\\n                      <div className=\\"truncate text-sm font-medium\\">{row.title}</div>\\r\\n                      <div className=\\"mt-0.5 text-[11px] text-muted-foreground\\">\\r\\n                        {row.kind === \'guided\' ? \'Guided drill\' : \'Activity\'} · {row.subtitle}\\r\\n                      </div>\\r\\n                    </div>\\r\\n                    <span className=\\"shrink-0 text-[10px] uppercase tracking-wider text-muted-foreground\\">\\r\\n                      {row.status.replace(\'_\', \' \')}\\r\\n                    </span>\\r\\n                  </li>\\r\\n                ))}\\r\\n              </ul>\\r\\n            ) : ownedWork.failedSources.length === 0 && !ownedWork.error ? (\\r\\n              <p className=\\"text-xs text-muted-foreground\\">No other guided drills or Activities.</p>\\r\\n            ) : null}\\r\\n          </>\\r\\n        )}\\r\\n      </section>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <HubAssignments studentId={studentId} />\\r\\n\\r\\n      {/* Renders nothing when there is no stored progress, so it never adds an\\r\\n          empty block. */}\\r\\n      <BootcampLessonProgress studentClassId={studentId} />\\r\\n\\r\\n      {/* What their Activity work says to teach next, read from the attempts\\r\\n          they have already made. Page through all matching rows so the default 1,000-row API limit\\r\\n * cannot make a later assignment look untouched.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function loadActivityWorkEvidence(studentId: string, assignmentIds: string[]): Promise<Set<string>> {\\r\\n  const found = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const ids = [...new Set(assignmentIds)];\\r\\n  const pageSize = 500;\\r\\n  for (let start = 0; start < ids.length; start += 50) {\\r\\n    const group = ids.slice(start, start + 50);\\r\\n    for (let offset = 0; ; offset += pageSize) {\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\\r\\n        .from(\'activity_item_responses\')\\r\\n        .select(\'id,assignment_id\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'student_id\', studentId)\\r\\n        .in(\'assignment_id\', group)\\r\\n        .order(\'id\', { ascending: true })\\r\\n        .range(offset, offset + pageSize - 1);\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n      const rows = data ?? [];\\r\\n      for (const row of rows) found.add(row.assignment_id);\\r\\n      if (rows.length < pageSize || group.every((id) => found.has(id))) break;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return found;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Implemented Activity parity in four files:

- Teacher mirrors use issued Activity source and saved-response evidence.
- Missing/failed evidence shows **Unverified**, never mirror completion.
- Activity Unassign is disabled with visible explanation; ordinary controls remain.

Nine tests passed. Changed-file ESLint passed. Typecheck is still running in session `95413`. Root must verify the teacher UI in the browser before release.

No live records changed. Genuine unassignment still needs an atomic backend contract; existing CLI only archives the Classroom mirror.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 12.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c22ab3","wall_time_seconds":3.1313661,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2324,"output":"/**\\r\\n * Handing one leg of a practice test to the runner that already exists, and\\r\\n * reading its result back.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The sitting owns sequence, clock and scoring. It does NOT own answering —\\r\\n * that stays in `/drill` (LR) and `/rc` (RC), so a question inside a practice\\r\\n * test behaves identically to the same question outside one. The cost of that\\r\\n * choice is this file: the runners report to their own tables, and the sitting\\r\\n * has to go and find the row afterwards.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * A leg is matched by \\"this user, this section, finished after the moment the\\r\\n * leg began\\". That is why `startedAt` is stamped before navigating and never\\r\\n * after.\\r\\n */\\r\\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\r\\nimport type { FullSectionConfig, TimerMode } from \'@/types/drill\';\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  practiceTestRunSectionSeconds,\\r\\n  type PracticeTestLeg,\\r\\n  type PracticeTestRun,\\r\\n} from \'@/lib/practiceTest\';\\r\\nimport { RC_STANDARD_SECONDS_PER_PASSAGE } from \'@/features/rc/lib/rcTypes\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst PRACTICE_TEST_PATH_PREFIX = \'/testing/test/\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function practiceTestPath(runId: string): string {\\r\\n  return `${PRACTICE_TEST_PATH_PREFIX}${runId}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Whether a return path points back into a sitting.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * ROUTING ONLY: it decides where a runner navigates and whether score UI is\\r\\n * suppressed. It must never gate a clock or an integrity rule, because the path\\r\\n * it reads is student-editable. Anything load-bearing reads persisted run and\\r\\n * leg attribution instead.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The prefix lives here, next to the function that writes it, so the two cannot\\r\\n * drift apart.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function isSittingReturn(path: string | null | undefined): boolean {\\r\\n  return !!path?.startsWith(PRACTICE_TEST_PATH_PREFIX);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The clock a leg runs on.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * This used to map the pace id straight onto an absolute TimerMode: \'standard\'\\r\\n * became \'35\' no matter what the student\'s standing timing mode said, so a\\r\\n * student carrying extra time who chose \\"Standard\\" was handed a 35-minute\\r\\n * section for every leg of a two-hour sitting. The seconds are resolved against\\r\\n * their own clock when the run is created, and read back here, so the pace is\\r\\n * relative for everyone. \'custom\' carries the exact figure, including the\\r\\n * fractional 52.5 and 78.75 minute cases, which the named modes cannot express.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction timerForRun(run: PracticeTestRun): { mode: TimerMode; customMinutes?: number } {\\r\\n  const seconds = practiceTestRunSectionSeconds(run);\\r\\n  if (seconds === null) return { mode: \'unlimited\' };\\r\\n  return { mode: \'custom\', customMinutes: seconds / 60 };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Which run and which leg a runner was launched for.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The runner needs this to key its closeout bookkeeping, and it cannot be\\r\\n * recovered from `returnTo`: that string is a routing hint, editable and\\r\\n * removable, and clock integrity must never depend on it. The leg INDEX is\\r\\n * passed rather than searched for, because `legs` can hold two legs of the same\\r\\n * section and matching by section number would pick the wrong one.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface PtLegIdentity {\\r\\n  runId: string;\\r\\n  legIndex: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrLegLaunch {\\r\\n  pathname: \'/drill\';\\r\\n  state: {\\r\\n    mode: \'full-section\';\\r\\n    config: FullSectionConfig;\\r\\n    returnTo: string;\\r\\n    practicingLabel: string;\\r\\n    ptRun: PtLegIdentity;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function lrLegLaunch(\\r\\n  run: PracticeTestRun,\\r\\n  leg: PracticeTestLeg,\\r\\n  legIndex: number,\\r\\n): LrLegLaunch {\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    pathname: \'/drill\',\\r\\n    state: {\\r\\n      mode: \'full-section\',\\r\\n      config: {\\r\\n        pt: run.pt,\\r\\n        section: leg.section,\\r\\n        timer: {\\r\\n          ...timerForRun(run),\\r\\n          isPaused: false,\\r\\n          elapsedMs: 0,\\r\\n          startedAt: Date.now(),\\r\\n        },\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      returnTo: practiceTestPath(run.id),\\r\\n      practicingLabel: `Practice Test ${run.pt}`,\\r\\n      ptRun: { runId: run.id, legIndex },\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The RC leg\'s link. `reveal=end_of_set` because a section is sat under test\\r\\n * conditions — nothing is revealed until the sitting is over, and inside a\\r\\n * practice test that is doubly true.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function rcLegHref(\\r\\n  run: PracticeTestRun,\\r\\n  leg: PracticeTestLeg,\\r\\n  legIndex: number,\\r\\n): string {\\r\\n  const secondsPerPassage = (() => {\\r\\n    // Same reading as the LR leg: the run\'s own resolved clock, so an\\r\\n    // accommodated student\'s RC passages get their share of the longer section\\r\\n    // rather than a share of 35 minutes.\\r\\n    const sectionSeconds = practiceTestRunSectionSeconds(run);\\r\\n    if (sectionSeconds === null) return null;\\r\\n    const passages = leg.passageIds?.length || 4;\\r\\n    return Math.round(sectionSeconds / passages);\\r\\n  })();\\r\\n\\r\\n  const params = new URLSearchParams({\\r\\n    passages: (leg.passageIds ?? []).join(\',\'),\\r\\n    reveal: \'end_of_set\',\\r\\n    timing: secondsPerPassage === null ? \'untimed\' : \'standard\',\\r\\n    start: \'1\',\\r\\n    returnTo: practiceTestPath(run.id),\\r\\n    ptRun: run.id,\\r\\n    ptLeg: String(legIndex),\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  if (secondsPerPassage !== null && secondsPerPassage !== RC_STANDARD_SECONDS_PER_PASSAGE) {\\r\\n    params.set(\'secondsPerPassage\', String(secondsPerPassage));\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return `/rc?${params.toString()}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Reopen the one RC session attached to this exam leg instead of inserting another. */\\r\\nexport async function findRcLegSession(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  runId: string,\\r\\n  legIndex: number,\\r\\n): Promise<{ id: string; status: string } | null> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await supabase.from(\'rc_drill_sessions\')\\r\\n    .select(\'id,status\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'practice_test_run_id\', runId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'practice_test_leg\', legIndex)\\r\\n    .maybeSingle();\\r\\n  if (error) throw error;\\r\\n  return data;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LegResult {\\r\\n  correct: number;\\r\\n  answered: number;\\r\\n  timeMs: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Read one finished LR leg out of `section_history`.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Returns null when no matching row exists yet, which is the normal answer for\\r\\n * a leg the student walked away from rather than completed. The caller decides\\r\\n * what to do about that; this never invents a zero, because a zero here reads\\r\\n * as \\"answered everything wrong\\" on the score report.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function readLrLegResult(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  pt: number,\\r\\n  section: number,\\r\\n  startedAtIso: string,\\r\\n  identity?: PtLegIdentity,\\r\\n): Promise<LegResult | null> {\\r\\n  let query = supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'section_history\')\\r\\n    .select(\'initial_score, initial_total, total_questions, total_time_ms, time_taken_ms, completed_at\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'pt\', pt)\\r\\n    .eq(\'section\', section);\\r\\n  query = identity\\r\\n    ? query.eq(\'practice_test_run_id\', identity.runId).eq(\'practice_test_leg\', identity.legIndex)\\r\\n    : query.gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso);\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await query\\r\\n    .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\\r\\n    .limit(1);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (error || !data || data.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n  const row = data[0];\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    correct: row.initial_score ?? 0,\\r\\n    answered: row.initial_total ?? row.total_questions ?? 0,\\r\\n    timeMs: row.total_time_ms ?? row.time_taken_ms ?? 0,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Read one finished RC leg out of `rc_drill_sessions` + `rc_attempts`.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Matched on the passage set rather than a section number: RC sessions record\\r\\n * which passages were sat, not which section they came from.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function readRcLegResult(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  passageIds: number[],\\r\\n  startedAtIso: string,\\r\\n  identity?: PtLegIdentity,\\r\\n): Promise<LegResult | null> {\\r\\n  let query = supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'rc_drill_sessions\')\\r\\n    .select(\'id, passage_ids, completed_at\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'status\', \'completed\');\\r\\n  query = identity\\r\\n    ? query.eq(\'practice_test_run_id\', identity.runId).eq(\'practice_test_leg\', identity.legIndex)\\r\\n    : query.gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso);\\r\\n  const { data: sessions, error } = await query\\r\\n    .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\\r\\n    .limit(5);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (error || !sessions || sessions.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const wanted = [...passageIds].sort((a, b) => a - b).join(\',\');\\r\\n  const session = sessions.find(\\r\\n    (s) => [...(s.passage_ids ?? ## assistant

RC readback fixed:

- `selected` is the answer column. Only A-E selections count as answered.
- Saved session clock determines section time.
- Failed attempt reads cannot produce a score.
- Noncurrent closeout rows now write zero question time instead of repeating the visible question’s elapsed time.

30 focused tests passed. Final ESLint rerun is session `33591`; integrated typecheck/build and browser verification remain with root.

Existing live attempts and cached parent results were preserved. No commit or push.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## assistant

`ActivityRunner.tsx:465` trusts `status === "completed"` alone. With no completion timestamp or saved responses, it opens an empty summary showing all 48 items unchecked.

Next fix: require confirmed completion consistently for routing, stored-score display (`:751`), and hydration errors (`:408`). Otherwise restore intro or saved progress. Fully resolved responses should show completion only after the account confirms the completion write.

No code or fixture changes.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"15c3c9","wall_time_seconds":0.2149125,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":5119,"output":"# Timed work must commit through one account transaction\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-09-30\\r\\n\\r\\nStatus: **Proposed implementation contract. Not implemented or deployed.** This is the backend follow-up for competitor-audit ticket #167. It does not close #167 or claim full second-device, outage, or revoked-access acceptance. The frontend recovery fixes can ship independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why the existing saves leave a race\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent PrepTest save already compares revision, active device, and in-progress status in one conditional UPDATE. Its LR and RC children check the parent separately before writing their own rows. A second browser can take over after that check and before the child write. Browser-local write queues do not serialize two devices. Ownership RLS prevents writing another student\'s work; it does not prevent an old browser from writing the same student\'s work.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvidence in this first table is from repository source. **SQL files describe intended schema, not verified live DDL.** The next section separately records a live metadata inspection on 2026-09-30. Neither metadata inspection nor a successful ordinary save proves a two-device race test passed. Record an independent authenticated CAS test before rollout.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Source | Existing behavior and limitation |\\r\\n| --- | --- |\\r\\n| `src/lib/practiceTestStore.ts:271`, `:302`, `:244` | `saveAccountPracticeTestRun` conditionally writes parent revision/device/status; `continueAccountPracticeTestHere` claims the device with parent CAS. `isActivePracticeTestDevice` is a separate read used by child writers. |\\r\\n| `src/lib/drillResumeSession.ts:56`, `:76` | `assertActiveTestLeg` checks the parent device; `syncSectionToAccount` separately upserts the LR frozen snapshot into `bootcamp_kv`. Official section writes have local serialization and recovery copies. |\\r\\n| `src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts:93`, `:154`, `:228`, `:184` | `assertActivePracticeTestSession`, `writeSessionClock`, `recordAttempt`, and `completeSession` use separate parent reads and child writes. RC clock serialization is per browser. |\\r\\n| `src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx:380`, `:388` | The parent stores break start time; failed-save recovery freezes remaining time locally. The break heartbeat currently runs at 15 seconds. |\\r\\n| `src/components/AssignmentGate.tsx:32`, `:299` | Route admission accepts a feature flag or item authorization, with cached assignment reads. It is not a server write contract and cannot provide immediate revocation enforcement. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260829120000_practice_test_runs.sql:135`, `:237`, `:245`, `:249`, `:281` | Parent owner UPDATE, RC owner FK, one LR result/RC session per leg, and RC first-attempt uniqueness. Owner UPDATE does not enforce terminal transitions, device ownership, or current access. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260927010000_practice_test_account_sync.sql:3` | Adds `section_seconds` and `active_device_id`. Does not add a clock lease or transactional child writes. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260923010300_practice_test_assignments.sql:41`, `:63` | Defines `can_sit_practice_test` and applies it to parent INSERT. Existing owner UPDATE remains broader. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260603142340_2aa5f9d2-63ec-4736-af91-7a426457f9de.sql:31` | KV owner UPDATE permits account-owned JSON snapshots without active-leg or lease checks. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260806000000_rc_drilling.sql:35` | RC owner policies scope work to the owner. They do not implement clock/device/access state transitions. |\\r\\n\\r\\nReuse these tables and constraints. The existing `abandon_stale_practice_tests` RPC is cleanup, not an atomic checkpoint API. No relevant timed checkpoint RPC was found in these files.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Live metadata confirms the child-write gap\\r\\n\\r\\nOn **2026-09-30**, read-only Lovable `query_database` SELECTs inspected PostgreSQL catalogs for project `53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`. Database status was enabled with Supabase stack. The queries read schema metadata only: no student rows, seeds, credentials, DDL, or mutations. They covered `pg_policies`, `information_schema.columns`, `pg_constraint`, `pg_indexes`, non-internal `pg_trigger`, relevant `pg_proc`, RLS flags, and ordinary-role table grants.\\r\\n\\r\\nConfirmed installed:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `practice_test_runs` has `revision integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, nullable `active_device_id text`, nullable positive `section_seconds integer`, break fields, and existing parent status constraints. No lease/epoch/clock-state/contract-version columns were present.\\r\\n- `bootcamp_kv` has exactly owner, namespace, record ID, JSON data, and updated timestamp columns. There is no separate writer revision/lease field.\\r\\n- RC sessions have parent run/leg attribution and nullable completion time. RC attempts have UUID primary key, session/owner/question identity and first-attempt flag. Neither table has a writer revision/epoch/lease column.\\r\\n- Composite ownership FKs `rc_sessions_pt_run_fk`, `rc_attempts_session_owner_fk`, and `section_history_pt_run_fk` are installed. RC and section-history attributed-leg pair checks are installed.\\r\\n- Unique partial indexes `rc_sessions_pt_leg_once`, `section_history_pt_leg_once`, and `rc_attempts_first_once` are installed. These prevent duplicate leg rows/first-attempt rows, not stale writer acceptance.\\r\\n- RLS is enabled on all four timed-progress tables. It is not FORCE RLS. Authenticated has INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/SELECT table privileges; policies determine which ordinary API operations succeed. Anonymous also has table grants, but owner checks still depend on `auth.uid()`; grants alone do not prove anonymous access.\\r\\n- The only non-internal triggers on the four progress tables and `section_history` are `bootcamp_kv_touch` and `practice_test_runs_touch`, both updated-timestamp triggers. No active-writer/access/terminal-state trigger was installed on these tables.\\r\\n- `can_sit_practice_test(_user uuid, _pt integer)` is installed as STABLE SECURITY DEFINER, checking admin, `profiles.has_practice_access`, or an owned nonarchived/unlocked exact PT assignment. `can_access_drill` was absent. The relevant function-name and function-body reference scan returned `can_sit_practice_test` and `abandon_stale_practice_tests`, plus timestamp triggers in the name scan; no checkpoint API appeared in that scoped inventory.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following policy text is the narrow raw evidence returned by `pg_policies`. SQL formatting is preserved; `NULL` means that catalog field was null. Read policies are summarized afterward because they are not the mutation blocker.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Table / policy / command / roles | `qual` | `with_check` |\\r\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\r\\n| `practice_test_runs` / `pt_runs_owner_insert` / INSERT / `{public}` | NULL | `((user_id = auth.uid()) AND can_sit_practice_test(auth.uid(), pt))` |\\r\\n| `practice_test_runs` / `pt_runs_owner_update` / UPDATE / `{public}` | `(user_id = auth.uid())` | `(user_id = auth.uid())` |\\r\\n| `bootcamp_kv` / `Users insert own bootcamp data` / INSERT / `{authenticated}` | NULL | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` |\\r\\n| `bootcamp_kv` / `Users update own bootcamp data` / UPDATE / `{authenticated}` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` |\\r\\n| `bootcamp_kv` / `Admins delete bootcamp data` / DELETE / `{authenticated}` | `has_role(auth.uid(), \'admin\'::app_role)` | NULL |\\r\\n| `rc_drill_sessions` / `own sessions` / ALL / `{public}` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` |\\r\\n| `rc_attempts` / `own attempts` / ALL / `{public}` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` | `(auth.uid() = user_id)` |\\r\\n\\r\\nOwn SELECT policies are installed for parent/KV. Admin SELECT policies named `admins_read_practice_test_runs`, `admins_read_bootcamp_kv`, `admins_read_rc_drill_sessions`, and `admins_read_rc_attempts` use `has_role(auth.uid(), \'admin\'::app_role)`. RC owner ALL policies include SELECT. No parent DELETE policy was returned.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Confirmed blocker:** live owner UPDATE/RC ALL policies contain no current-access, active-device, active-leg, revision, or terminal-state predicates. No relevant enforcement trigger supplies them. Existing ownership FKs and uniqueness constraints are stronger than a client-only ownership convention but do not close the parent-check/child-write race. Parent CAS remains implemented by the caller\'s conditional UPDATE, not required by a database policy. This establishes the live schema gap; it does not claim successful denial/race/revocation mutation tests.\\r\\n\\r\\n## One versioned command owns parent and child writes\\r\\n\\r\\nImplement `commit_timed_work_v1` as a database transaction. For a PrepTest, lock its existing `practice_test_runs` parent row. For standalone timed LR/RC, lock a small normalized `timed_work_controls` row keyed to the immutable owned LR snapshot or RC session identity. This companion row prevents generic KV JSON from becoming the source of device ownership or source authorization.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe command accepts a work identity, expected revision, writer epoch/token, command UUID, operation, and a typed operation payload. It does not accept an arbitrary student owner. Derive the actor from `auth.uid()`.\\r\\n\\r\\nAllowed operations: claim, start, checkpoint, pause, resume, complete, abandon; PrepTests additionally start/finish break and complete/advance leg. Validate each operation separately rather than accepting an unrestricted parent/JSON patch.\\r\\n\\r\\nWithin the transaction:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Lock the control/parent row for `auth.uid()` and validate contract version.\\r\\n2. Check current access against live source rows, expected revision, active writer epoch, allowed state transition, and exact current leg.\\r\\n3. Check child ownership and immutable source attribution. A PrepTest child must belong to this parent, owner, leg, section kind, and frozen section definition.\\r\\n4. Update the official LR frozen snapshot or the RC attempt, elapsed clock, and current position together with the control state. Reject missing/unavailable question identities rather than drawing a new queue.\\r\\n5. On completion, acknowledge the official result and advance the parent atomically. No next leg or final score becomes available before this transaction commits.\\r\\n6. Increment revision, store the command receipt, and return the authoritative control/parent plus child checkpoint and server time.\\r\\n\\r\\nMaintain durable command receipts keyed by owner, work identity, and command UUID, with a payload hash and committed result. Same UUID/same payload returns the acknowledged result after a lost response. Same UUID/different payload is rejected. Check a matching receipt before rejecting a replay for stale revision: a legitimately committed command remains acknowledged after later operations. A new stale command never overwrites newer work.\\r\\n\\r\\nLR analytics and WAJ remain projections of acknowledged work. If they cannot be written inside closeout, enqueue idempotent follow-up work transactionally and keep retries independent of whether the official leg can advance. RC attempts retain existing UUIDs and first-attempt uniqueness. Decide explicitly which projections must finish before results appear; do not silently weaken the current closeout contract.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Every save rechecks access without changing the actor\\r\\n\\r\\nPrepTest commands reuse `can_sit_practice_test(auth.uid(), parent.pt)`, including current unlock/archive state and the Practice tests feature flag. Validate the active exact leg independently of the feature flag. A broad feature grant does not authorize an arbitrary child under a different parent or leg.\\r\\n\\r\\nStandalone LR/RC control creation freezes its source discriminator and source identity. Each protected write requires the current drill feature grant or a current, owned assignment that authorizes exactly that section/session source. Validate unlock/archive/deletion state and source membership server-side. Opening unrelated assigned work must not grant access to arbitrary sections. Do not infer authorization from a caller-supplied queue, generic KV namespace, or cached route result.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe standalone source mapping must be inventoried and implemented for every supported launch before enforcement is enabled. Unsupported source kinds fail explicitly. Teacher/admin read access continues through existing SELECT policies. No teacher mutation impersonates the student\'s writer; any future teacher recovery operation needs its own audited authorization contract.\\r\\n\\r\\nAccess revocation denies new checkpoint, resume, claim, and completion commands. Preserve prior answers and recovery copies. Historical owned result reads remain available according to the existing result-access policy. Retrying an already committed command can acknowledge its existing receipt but cannot create further work after revocation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The server needs a clock and an expiring writer lease\\r\\n\\r\\nStore phase, accumulated elapsed milliseconds, server running anchor, lease expiry, writer epoch, and contract version. For PrepTests the clock state identifies the exact leg or break; section duration remains the frozen `section_seconds` chosen at creation. Standalone controls hold their own frozen timing. Use server time for accepted state transitions; never replace saved time with a smaller client value.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Proposed, not locked:** heartbeat every 15 seconds, writer lease 45 seconds. This follows the current break heartbeat cadence and tolerates two missed heartbeats before expiry. It bounds stale-writer acceptance and server-visible disconnect behavior without treating a single transient request failure as a permanent takeover. Verify the tolerance against real latency and teaching requirements before adopting it.\\r\\n\\r\\nA successful pause checkpoints elapsed time and removes the running anchor. Resume creates a new anchor only after acknowledgment. When the lease expires, the authoritative clock stops at its expiry boundary and the work requires explicit recovery/claim. A claim increments the writer epoch so all old in-flight writes are rejected under the same parent lock.\\r\\n\\r\\n**An offline pause that never reaches the server cannot be globally instantaneous.** Another device can only observe the last acknowledged checkpoint and lease expiry. Document that interval in acceptance evidence. A local paused clock remains a recovery copy, not proof that the server paused at the same instant. Reconcile unacknowledged elapsed time deliberately and monotonically; do not auto-replay local answers over a newer writer\'s work.\\r\\n\\r\\nAn explicit takeover may invalidate an unexpired lease if the existing UX permits it. The command must still CAS the latest revision and increment the epoch atomically. Expiry must never reset timing or grant a fresh question queue.\\r\\n\\r\\n## RPC adoption must also close direct-write bypasses\\r\\n\\r\\nFor protected contract-version rows, deny authenticated direct parent UPDATE and direct writes to attributed LR KV records, RC session/attempt rows, and official section results. Cover INSERT as well as UPDATE so a caller cannot recreate a child under a new identity. Apply the rule to the OLD row as well as NEW attribution so clearing a protected foreign key cannot bypass enforcement.\\r\\n\\r\\nAn RPC alone is insufficient while broad owner policies still allow the same writes. Permissive policies are ORed; inspect and replace conflicting policies rather than adding a stricter policy beside them. Keep ordinary non-timed/non-protected bootcamp progress behavior intact.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse tightly scoped security-definer functions only where required to perform these protected writes. Restrict execution to authenticated callers, fix the search path, qualify objects, and explicitly verify `auth.uid()`, ownership, source access, writer epoch, and operation payload. No service-role credential goes to the browser. A transaction-local enforcement mechanism, if used, must not be forgeable by authenticated callers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Roll out without erasing old sittings\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Refresh the live metadata inventory recorded above immediately before deployment. Verify the existing parent CAS with two ordinary authenticated clients. Record differences from repository SQL before applying changes.\\r\\n2. Add nullable/versioned clock state, standalone control rows, command receipts, and RPCs. Leave historical completed records readable and do not rewrite their timing or answers.\\r\\n3. Add frontend adapters that consume authoritative RPC responses. New timed work uses contract v1. Existing active work enters through an explicit adoption/claim transaction that preserves owner, frozen queue, acknowledged answers, current position, accommodation timing, and completion state.\\r\\n4. Enable protected direct-write restrictions for v1 work. Old browser clients encountering adopted work must fail safely and require refresh; they cannot keep using the legacy writer.\\r\\n5. Complete race, outage, revocation, and compatibility acceptance before widening adoption. No terminal-state reopening, deletion, synthetic timestamps, or silent conversion of an unknown-owner device copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nLegacy active work still using the old protocol does not carry the new cross-device guarantee. Keep #167 open until its supported active paths have adopted the contract or are explicitly excluded with truthful UI behavior. Deploy forward; never roll main back to bypass enforcement.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Acceptance proves the transaction rather than the happy path\\r\\n\\r\\n| Scenario | Required result |\\r\\n| --- | --- |\\r\\n| Two clients submit the same expected revision | Exactly one new command commits; the other gets conflict with authoritative state. |\\r\\n| Takeover races an LR/RC save | Parent lock and epoch decide order; no old-writer child commit after successful takeover. |\\r\\n| Direct protected INSERT/UPDATE or attribution clearing | Denied for ordinary authenticated owner and other account. |\\r\\n| Old epoch, wrong owner/source/leg, terminal parent | No child or parent mutation; saved work remains intact. |\\r\\n| Access is revoked after route admission | New checkpoint/claim/resume/completion denied despite cached route authorization. |\\r\\n| Response lost after commit | Same command UUID/payload returns one acknowledged answer/position/result without duplicate advancement. |\\r\\n| Replayed UUID with changed payload | Rejected; acknowledged receipt and result unchanged. |\\r\\n| A later valid command precedes retry of an old receipt | Old committed operation is acknowledged without restoring its older state. |\\r\\n| RC first-attempt insert races | One first attempt per session/question, UUID retries preserve the original answer. |\\r\\n| Pause/break save fails, reload, then second-device claim | Account clock/lease state is consistent; local backup cannot silently reset remaining time. |\\r\\n| Unsent offline pause | Server stops at documented expiry boundary; no claim of immediate global pause. |\\r\\n| Lost heartbeat and stale browser return | Questions remain gated until claim/recovery acknowledgment; elapsed time does not decrease. |\\r\\n| Leg closeout or final-score write fails midway | No partial advance/score; retry finishes the same leg exactly once. |\\r\\n| Older frontend resumes adopted work | Protected raw writes fail safely; refresh preserves acknowledged records. |\\r\\n| Teacher views records | Same owner/source work is visible through allowed reads; teacher identity never becomes the student writer. |\\r\\n\\r\\nTest standalone LR and standalone RC separately from PrepTest LR/RC and the break. Use existing owned tester fixtures, ordinary authenticated accounts, and controlled request failures. Do not substitute service-role success for permission evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Deployment needs immediate route and credit approval\\r\\n\\r\\nThis batch contains the implementation plan only. It does not contain schema changes, RPC code, frontend RPC adapters, or a backend deployment.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore starting a Lovable Cloud backend deployment, confirm the deployment route with Joshua. Deploy managed Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has been migrated to a separately managed project. Any action that spends or may spend Lovable credits needs Joshua\'s explicit confirmation immediately before that action, even though frontend releases were broadly authorized. Free publication controls do not authorize a credit-using backend action.\\r\\n\\r\\nPrepare the reviewed migration/RPC code and test evidence first so that approval concerns a concrete deployment. Generate database types from the deployed backend afterward; do not hand-edit generated types. Report separately: code prepared, backend deployed, frontend switched, live acceptance verified.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text':
